## Package questions

No maintainer-authored probing questions were supplied. Explore the live source freely; shadow inputs are orientation only.

## How to hunt one file
Each item uses one source file as its entry point into the live system. The
target is narrow; the investigation and root-cause fix may cross files.

- Read the shadow overview and stripped source for baseline orientation, then
  read the complete live target. Shadow markers and stripped line numbers are
  never authoritative.
- Trace callers, dependencies, state, framework hooks, tests, examples, and
  public contracts far enough to understand the target's real behavior. Clean
  layers often fail only when several reasonable assumptions stack together;
  hunt those interactions, not only suspicious local lines.
- Break things, break things, break things. Write messy scratch test files and
  be maximally destructive inside disposable scratch scope: mutate throwaway
  state, force hostile sequences, interrupt lifecycles, and try to make every
  connected layer fail.
- For every extreme, test the opposite extreme and then combine them across
  layers. Try to disprove every candidate and record only confirmed defects.
- Do not clean up scratch probes or disposable state. Report every path and
  leave it intact so Worker 0 can independently verify it and clean it up only
  after the item passes.
- Implement the root-cause fix at the layer that owns the broken invariant,
  including connected files when required. Add a permanent behavioral test for
  every production fix at the strongest tier required by `AGENTS.md`.
- After edits run `uv run ruff format .` and `uv run ruff check --fix .`.
- Report evidence, changed files, tests, and validation to Worker 0. Do not edit
  this progress file; Worker 0 independently verifies fixes and advances it.

All five axes discharged by executed probes: (1) shape/container - handoff is exactly `(mount, set-up instance)`, consumed exactly once via delattr, every non-tuple shape (None/bool/str/list/tuple-subclass/foreign-mount) falls back un-consumed, `update_wrapper` preserves the handoff, a wrapper that drops the marks degrades to view-local arrangement (never silently reordered); (2) absent vs null through a real middleware call - absent/None/False/0/"" keep the exemption True, True and truthy non-bool withdraw it, a stamp on an unpublished request changes nothing, the answer is computed fresh per read; (3) lexical - exact distinct mark strings pinned, trailing-space/case-drift lookalikes do NOT withdraw the exemption, a one-letter-short near-miss boundary name is declined; (4) hostile callables - hostile metaclass reads, hostile descriptor under the probed name, hostile callback `__getattr__` all DECLINE (None, never a raise); ValueError out of construction propagates deliberately (the TypeError arm means "not buildable", not "swallow everything"); a recognized boundary raising raw exceptions escapes deliberately (documented decision); a raising mount read cannot undo a completed boundary; (5) absent-vs-empty config - shipped chain builds, empty chain accepted, missing-either-entry accepted, first-CSRF-entry rule pinned (parametrized misordered chains refuse at startup), function-middleware entries skipped in both orders, absent MIDDLEWARE -> raw AttributeError at chain build matching Django's own handler failure mode (documented). ContextVar lifecycle: publish/reset on sync and async, reset on downstream raise on both transports, nested inner calls restore the outer request, concurrent asyncio tasks cannot see each other's stamp, a spawned task DOES see the handling request (threadlocal would not), a worker thread answers "no boundary here".

    - Revision done (Worker 1, 2026-09-01): `test_probe_concurrency.py` rewritten honestly in place (the four prior-cycle probe files were in fact still on disk; Worker 0's empty-scope reading was a tool error and the correction is recorded here). No production or permanent-test edits in this revision. Honest invariant REPLACES the retracted "converges to installed" claim: (1) a bare `importlib.reload()` while a generation is installed preserves the RUNNING generation's validated teardown (no `RuntimeError` on subsequent teardown); (2) `_patch_is_installed()` is generation-bound (False after a bare reload is expected, not breakage) and one explicit `apply()` self-heals to the current generation; (3) a concurrent third-party write of `SimpleTestCase._remove_databases_failures` may legitimately hold the final write - the module guarantees self-heal at the next `apply()`, not continuous ownership. Dispatch-brief correction: `SimpleTestCase._databases_failures_support` does not exist on any supported Django (verified absent on 6.1 and in the audited 5.2.16-6.0.x shapes); the owned attribute is `SimpleTestCase._remove_databases_failures`.
    - Verification (Worker 0, 2026-09-01): PASSED. Read the rewritten probe in full: no vacuous assertions, no dead code, duplicate definitions or `range(0)` no-op gone, save/restore in finally blocks, control probe forces the validated-source global to None and proves the detector genuinely fires (the pre-fix shape reproduced on demand), the race asserts only OBSERVED state (zero apply errors, zero teardown failures, final descriptor wholly one side's) and then self-heals. Independently reran the whole scratch battery: 12/12 passed. Grep-confirmed the brief correction: `_databases_failures_support` appears nowhere in the package; the owned attribute is `SimpleTestCase._remove_databases_failures`, exactly what the probes exercise. Worker 1's same-session cross-tier run (scratch + `tests/test_django_patches.py`) reported 34 passed. Scope confirmed: no production or permanent-test edits in this revision. Result: Fixed Medium (production fix), revision closed with the honest invariant.
    - Cleanup: Removed `docs/bug_hunt/temp-tests/django_patches/` (rewritten concurrency probe plus the retained prior-cycle probes); concurrent and unrelated work preserved.
    - Result: Fixed Medium. `importlib.reload()` re-executed `_validated_remove_databases_failures_source = None` while the PREVIOUS patch generation stayed installed on `SimpleTestCase`, so every subsequent `tearDownClass` raised `RuntimeError` until the next `apply()` - permanently if patches were disabled at that moment.     - Interim finding INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED by Worker 0 (2026-08-31), fix not yet implemented. Worker 0 reran `uv run pytest docs/bug_hunt/temp-tests/consumers/test_probe_hostile_frames.py --no-cov -n0 -q` and reproduced the reported result exactly: 18 failed / 3 passed, each failure carrying the raw exception from the connection task. Confirmed classes: non-object JSON frames (`[1,2,3]`, `null`, `42`, `"hello"`, `true`) raise `TypeError: list indices must be integers...` / `'NoneType' object is not subscriptable` / `'int' object is not subscriptable` out of the dispatch's `message["type"]` on BOTH subprotocols; an unhashable operation id raises `TypeError: cannot use 'list' as a dict key`; a non-dict subscribe/start payload raises `TypeError: string indices must be integers`; a missing payload raises `KeyError('payload')` on the legacy protocol (transport-ws contains it as a 4400 close); a legacy `stop` for an unknown id raises `KeyError('nope')`; and a deeply nested JSON document raises `RecursionError` out of `decode_json` inside the `async for` over `iter_json`, where no handler-level catch can see it. Impact reproduced: one hostile frame kills the message-loop task while the socket stays OPEN and is never closed, so every later frame is ignored - a client-controlled per-connection DoS with unbounded socket lifetime. Severity High. Root cause is that the upstream `BaseGraphQLTransportWSHandler` / `BaseGraphQLWSHandler` message loops are inherited unchanged by `build_revalidating_consumer_class`, which wraps only `handle_subscribe`/`handle_start` admission and the adapter `send_json`, so the "no client input may crash the connection task" invariant has no owner at the dispatch layer.
    - Cross-item note: this also refutes the `extensions/resource_policy.py` hunter's assertion that no transport can deliver a non-string `query` - the WebSocket path performs no type checking whatsoever before `payload["query"]`. Both items are being revised.
    - Fix implemented (Worker 1, 2026-08-31): `handle()` overrides added on both generated protocol handlers, containing any `Exception` escaping the message loop and closing with code `4400` / reason `"Failed to parse message"` via new `_contain_message_loop_failure`. Containment is `except Exception`, not `except BaseException` minus a hard-coded trio, so `CancelledError`, `KeyboardInterrupt`, `SystemExit`, and `GeneratorExit` all bypass by inheritance rather than a name list a future subclass could fall through. Fixed the pre-existing `tests/test_routers.py` syntax error (line 5891, malformed docstring quoting) and trailing commas.
    - Verification (Worker 0, 2026-08-31): PASSED. `tests/test_routers.py` parses cleanly and `uv run pytest tests/test_routers.py -n0 --no-cov -q` is 198/198 passed. Grep-confirmed `except Exception` at all three loop-guard sites (`consumers.py:1506,1598,1639`). `ruff check` clean on both files. Separately reviewed the pre-existing `_RevocationGatedWebSocketAdapter.send_json` `except BaseException` blocks (predate this hunt's baseline at commit `7542d45d`, confirmed via `git show`): judged BELOW the fix bar for this item - they are a deliberate fail-closed security gate (every arm either re-raises the same three process-control exceptions or revokes/closes the connection), not a client-triggerable dead-open-socket DoS like the loop guard was, so rewriting dozens of call sites is out of scope here; flagged to the hunter as an optional documented lead only.

(2) HOSTILE POLICY BREAKS THE FLOOR (fail-open): `_degraded` read `policy.message` via defaulted `getattr` (suppresses only `AttributeError`), so an `ErrorPolicy` subclass minted via `object.__new__` (isinstance-valid, validation skipped) with a raising `message` property raised out of `_degraded` -> out of `mask_execution_result`'s outer degrade -> through the teardown floor re-raise, leaving the ORIGINAL sensitive message on the wire; same shape for a raising `policy.enabled` at the gate. (3) LYING CONTAINER SKIPS MASKING: `if not errors` trusted a falsy-but-populated container (`__bool__` lying) and skipped classification entirely. AttributeError on a GraphQLError subclass) as absent -> deliberate-delivery branch -> the error's own message shipped. No consumers.py change needed: with gate and masker infallible the per-event seam (`_stop_aware_results`) inherits the guarantees. Permanent tier: 231 new lines in `tests/test_error_policy.py` - gate stays active when DEBUG deleted (both proxy cache AND wrapped holder) or the holder is unreadable; teardown with unreadable DEBUG masks normally AND keeps healthy data (message + 32-char correlation id asserted); hostile-message policy degrades to the package message with the traceback logged (caplog `exc_info` asserted); floor refuses non-string/empty messages (direct `_degraded` drive-through, parametrized); lying falsy-populated container masked with order/identity preserved (deliberate entry stays `is`-identical); raising `original_error` masked not trusted (`original_error is None` on the replacement); genuinely-empty-list still a no-op.

But raw-pk relations are a FIRST-CLASS, package-shipped representation (targeting a non-Relay type, decoded by the same `utils/write_values.py::decode_visible_relation_ids` compose as GlobalID relations), and their list items are NOT the `ID` scalar. Consequence: a raw-pk relation list gets the LOOSER membership bound (500 vs 200) and never contributes to the request-wide relation aggregate at all, so the documented bound "relation ids in one mutation field, and across the whole request" does not hold for a representation the package itself ships. Every one of those ids reaches a `pk__in` visibility query, which is precisely what the relation bound exists to cap. Severity Medium (resource-bound evasion; the membership bound still caps a single list, so it is not unbounded, but the aggregate is uncapped across fields). The fix belongs HERE - classification must key on whether the argument is a relation input (the bind already records `RELATION_MULTI` in the input field specs), not on the `ID` scalar name.

The `ID`-scalar-name rule is now the explicit FALLBACK only for writes the package did not generate (a plain `strawberry.Schema`). New permanent live rows in `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_resource_policy_api.py` cover raw-pk over-bound rejection across model/form/serializer/nested flavors, per-field reset, aggregate accumulation across two raw-pk fields, and a mixed raw-pk + GlobalID aggregate; a unit test in `tests/test_resource_policy.py` pins the ID-scalar fallback on a plain-schema mutation.

    - Result: Fixed High (claimed). A `Prefetch("items", queryset=<unrelated model>.objects.all())` over a coincidentally same-named FK sealed clean; Django (#37267, unfixed in 6.1) groups prefetch children by lookup name WITHOUT checking the child's model against the relation target, so the foreign table's rows populate the `items` relation and reach GraphQL without the related type's `get_queryset` visibility hook ever running on them. Fix: new `_prefetch_relation_target_or_none` resolves the lookup path segment-by-segment through `_meta.get_field` and `_sealed_prefetch_related_lookups` (now taking `parent_model`) fails the outer seal closed as `untrusted` when the child's model is neither the relation target nor a subclass of it; unresolvable paths fail OPEN to Django unchanged. Files changed: `django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py`, `tests/utils/test_querysets.py`.
    - Verification (Worker 0, 2026-08-31): PARTIAL - the defect and the fix's direction are CONFIRMED, but the fix is INCOMPLETE and the item returns to Worker 1. Worker 0 wrote `docs/bug_hunt/temp-tests/querysets/worker0_verify_prefetch_target.py` and did NOT temp-revert (nine sibling agents share this tree). Confirmed by independent probe: (a) the underlying Django behavior really is a leak - driving `prefetch_related_objects` directly with the hostile child populates `category.items` with `Property` rows, so the High severity is earned, not inflated; (b) the new guard does fail closed on the hostile single-segment and multi-segment shapes with the `untrusted` code; (c) the guard does NOT over-block any legitimate shape tested - exact model, `items__entries` multi-segment, plain string lookup, queryset-less `Prefetch`, `to_attr`, forward FK, and a proxy subclass all still seal.
    - Revision required (Worker 0, 2026-08-31): the guard resolves lookup paths with `_meta.get_field`, but a reverse relation declared WITHOUT `related_name` is prefetched through the `<model>_set` ACCESSOR while `_meta` registers it under the bare model name - so `_prefetch_relation_target_or_none` returns `None` for that spelling and the seal fails OPEN exactly where the leak is still reachable. Six such relations exist in this project's own installed apps (`ContentType.permission_set`, `ContentType.logentry_set`, `User.logentry_set`, `Group.user_set`, `Permission.user_set`, `Permission.group_set`). Worker 0 proved the gap is EXPLOITABLE end to end, not merely unresolved: `Permission.content_type` and `LogEntry.content_type` are both FKs to `ContentType` with no `related_name`, and `Prefetch("permission_set", queryset=LogEntry.objects.all())` is admitted by the seal (`sealed=True, defect=None`) while `ContentType.permission_set` fills with `LogEntry` rows - the identical #37267 leak in the one spelling the fix misses. The correction belongs in the same helper (resolve the accessor spelling too, e.g. via the reverse-relation accessor map, keeping the fail-open contract for genuinely unresolvable paths) and needs its own permanent test.
    - Revision also required: the fix corrupted the `_seal_or_defect` docstring - it now reads "or a a ``Prefetch`` in" (doubled article) and repeats the two-line sentence "class-level method inventory: ``untrusted`` now means ... not \"overrides a listed method\"." twice in a row.
    - Iteration 2 (2026-08-31): Worker 1 closed the accessor spelling in the SAME helper. `_prefetch_relation_target_or_none` now tries `_meta.get_field` first and falls back to a new `_reverse_relation_by_accessor_or_none`, which matches `get_accessor_name()` across `_meta.get_fields()` for non-concrete reverse relations, so the default `<model>_set` spelling proves its target exactly as a `related_name` spelling does - including as an INTERMEDIATE segment. Four permanent tests added, notably `test_prefetch_child_default_accessor_wrong_model_fails_closed` (the real ContentType/LogEntry exploit shape) and `test_prefetch_relation_target_resolves_every_installed_default_accessor`, a project-wide sweep over `apps.get_models()` so no future app relation can silently re-open the class. Reverse M2M and reverse OneToOne accessor spellings were confirmed under the walk. Both docstring corruptions fixed.
